==============================================
ASGAYA QUICK START GUIDE
==============================================

AI-Optimized Overview (~15 minute read)
Version: 1.0
Last Updated: 2026-05-05

==============================================
WHAT IS ASGAYA?
==============================================

Asgaya is a permissionless, peer-to-peer remittance protocol that enables cross-border money
transfers using Bitcoin Cash as the settlement layer, with fiat on/off ramps through local
payment systems.

**Tagline:** Bitcoin Cash remittances with <1% fees, no KYC, self-custody

**Key Innovation:** Two-step settlement with pull-based BCH purchases eliminates volatility risk


CORE REQUIREMENTS
------------------

Asgaya is designed to satisfy three fundamental requirements:

1. CHEAPER THAN LEGACY (<1% fees)
   - Traditional remittances: 6.49% average cost
   - Asgaya target: <1% through market-rate exchanges + free P2P rails
   - On €100 transfer: Save €5.49

2. PROMOTE MERCHANT ADOPTION
   - Every transaction creates economic incentives for participants
   - Merchants earn ~0.247% per transaction
   - Liquidity Providers earn ~0.247% per settlement
   - Self-sustaining network growth

3. PERMISSIONLESS
   - No KYC requirements
   - Minimal hardware (Android phone)
   - Minimal knowledge (if you can use WhatsApp, you can use Asgaya)
   - Self-custody (users control their BCH)


FEE STRUCTURE
-------------

Total fee: 1% of transfer amount

On a €100 transfer, the 1% (€1.00) is distributed as:
- Exchange fee (Kraken): ~€0.26 (0.26%)
- Escrow operator: ~€0.247 (0.247%)
- Merchant: ~€0.247 (0.247%)
- Liquidity Provider: ~€0.247 (0.247%) - if instant settlement selected

How it works: The 1% fee first covers the Kraken exchange cost (~0.26%), then the
remaining ~0.74% is split equally three ways among the participants.

Comparison: €1.00 (Asgaya) vs €6.49 (traditional) = 6.5× cheaper


CURRENT STATUS
--------------

Phase: Public Beta (Seeking External Review)
Implementation: Documentation complete, code not started
Review Status: All critical gaps resolved (DeepSeek review - May 2026)

Next: Phase 0 implementation (Asgaya Husk - manual everything)

==============================================
THE PROBLEM
==============================================

REMITTANCE CRISIS
-----------------

Global remittances: $685 billion annually
Average cost: 6.49%
Money lost to fees: $44.5 billion per year

Despite UN and G20 pledges to reduce costs to 3%, the reality is stark:
over $44.5 billion never reaches its destination.


WHY ARE REMITTANCES EXPENSIVE?
-------------------------------

1. WALLED GARDENS
   - Payment systems don't interoperate (Bizum can't send to PagoMóvil)
   - Each system has monopoly within its jurisdiction
   - No competition = high fees

2. BUREAUCRACY
   - KYC/AML compliance costs
   - Multiple intermediaries
   - Regulatory overhead in each jurisdiction

3. VOLATILITY RISK
   - Previous crypto remittance attempts failed due to volatility
   - Price changes between send and receive destroyed value
   - Users lost trust after bad experiences

4. BOOTSTRAPPING PROBLEM
   - Merchants won't join without users
   - Users won't join without merchants
   - Chicken-and-egg deadlock


PREVIOUS CRYPTO ATTEMPTS (AND WHY THEY FAILED)
-----------------------------------------------

Bitcoin, Ethereum, Stellar, Ripple, etc. all tried crypto remittances.
They all failed for the same reason: VOLATILITY.

Scenario:
- Day 1: Sender sends €100, buys 0.01 BTC
- Day 5: Price drops 10%
- Day 5: Recipient cashes out, gets €90

Result: Recipient lost €10 due to volatility, not fees.

**Critical insight:** Volatility protection isn't optional, it's foundational.

==============================================
THE SOLUTION
==============================================

ASGAYA'S APPROACH
-----------------

Asgaya solves the four problems:

1. WALLED GARDENS → Use BCH as bridge
   - Bizum (Spain) → BCH → PagoMóvil (Venezuela)
   - Free P2P rails on both sides
   - BCH handles cross-border settlement

2. BUREAUCRACY → Permissionless design
   - No KYC (use existing payment apps)
   - No intermediaries (direct peer-to-peer)
   - No special licenses needed

3. VOLATILITY → Two-step settlement with pull system
   - EUR held until recipient claims (0% volatility)
   - BCH purchase only when ready to settle (30-second window)
   - Volatility risk reduced from 20 minutes to 30 seconds

4. BOOTSTRAPPING → Economic incentives
   - Merchants earn €0.247 per €100 transaction
   - Build network by mobilizing lost fees
   - Every transaction creates a new merchant advocate


TWO-STEP SETTLEMENT (THE KEY INNOVATION)
-----------------------------------------

Traditional crypto remittance:
1. Sender buys crypto
2. Crypto sits (volatility risk!)
3. Recipient sells crypto
4. Total volatility window: Hours or days

Asgaya's approach:
1. Sender sends EUR to escrow (0% volatility - it's fiat!)
2. EUR sits in escrow (no BCH bought yet)
3. Recipient goes to merchant, ready to claim
4. Escrow buys BCH (30-second transaction)
5. BCH settles to merchant
6. Merchant gives cash to recipient
7. Total BCH volatility window: 30 seconds

Result: 99.5% reduction in volatility exposure


PULL SYSTEM
-----------

Key principle: Don't buy BCH until someone is ready to receive fiat.

Push system (traditional):
- Sender triggers BCH purchase
- BCH sits waiting for recipient
- Volatility risk: High

Pull system (Asgaya):
- Recipient triggers BCH purchase (by claiming at merchant)
- BCH purchased and settled immediately
- Volatility risk: Minimal

Analogy: Don't make the sandwich until the customer walks in the door.

==============================================
HOW IT WORKS (STEP-BY-STEP)
==============================================

PARTICIPANTS
------------

1. SENDER (María, Barcelona)
   - Has EUR in Spanish bank account
   - Has Bizum app on phone
   - Wants to send €100 to Venezuela

2. RECIPIENT (Elena, Caracas)
   - Has Venezuelan phone number
   - Needs to receive bolívares (VES)
   - Doesn't need bank account or special app

3. MERCHANT (Pulpería owner, Caracas)
   - Has Android phone
   - Has Asgaya app installed
   - Has cash available (bolívares)
   - Earns ~€0.247 per transaction

4. LIQUIDITY PROVIDER (Optional)
   - Has EUR and BCH
   - Provides instant settlement
   - Earns ~€0.247 per settlement

5. ESCROW OPERATOR
   - Holds EUR temporarily
   - Coordinates BCH purchase
   - Earns ~€0.247 per transaction


THE FLOW (EUR → VES REMITTANCE)
--------------------------------

STEP 1: SENDER INITIATES (María in Barcelona)
- Opens Asgaya app
- Enters: €100 to send
- Enters: Elena's Venezuelan phone (0412-XXX-5678)
- Enters: Recipient name (Elena)
- App calculates: 4,999,500 VES will arrive (using live rates)
- App shows fee: €1.00 total
- María approves

STEP 2: SENDER PAYS ESCROW (via Bizum)
- App generates unique code: ASG-7X2K9
- María opens Bizum app
- Sends €100 to escrow's Bizum account
- Concept field: "ASG-7X2K9 Elena 0412-XXX-5678"
- Payment completes instantly (Bizum is free and instant in Spain)

STEP 3: ESCROW RECEIVES & HOLDS
- Escrow's NotificationListener detects Bizum payment
- Parses concept field, extracts code + recipient phone
- Matches payment to María's pending transaction
- EUR sits in escrow (NO BCH PURCHASED YET - this is key!)
- Sends SMS to Elena: "María sent you 4,999,500 VES. Code: ASG-7X2K9. Go to merchant."

STEP 4: RECIPIENT GOES TO MERCHANT (Elena in Caracas)
- Elena receives SMS notification
- Goes to nearby pulpería (small neighborhood store)
- Merchant has Asgaya app installed

STEP 5: MERCHANT ENTERS CODE
- Merchant opens Asgaya app
- Enters code: ASG-7X2K9
- App shows: "Elena, 4,999,500 VES"
- Merchant confirms details match

STEP 6: CASH-OUT (THE CRITICAL MOMENT)
- Merchant gives Elena 4,999,500 VES in cash
- Elena confirms receipt in merchant's app (face-to-face)
- Merchant confirms delivery in app

STEP 7: PULL SYSTEM TRIGGERS (BCH Settlement)
- ONLY NOW does escrow buy BCH (pull-based!)
- Escrow sends Bizum to Kraken: €99.74 (€100 - €0.26 Kraken fee)
- Kraken instantly converts to BCH at market rate
- BCH sent to merchant's wallet
- Merchant receives: ~€0.247 in BCH (their fee)
- LP receives: ~€0.247 in BCH (if instant settlement)
- Escrow keeps: ~€0.247 (their fee)

Total BCH volatility window: ~30 seconds (Kraken exchange + BCH transaction)

STEP 8: COMPLETION
- María sees: "Delivered" status
- Elena has: Cash in hand
- Merchant has: BCH reward + new customer
- System has: New trusted merchant node


WHAT IF RECIPIENT DOESN'T CLAIM?
---------------------------------

Unclaimed transaction policy:
- Claim window: 24 hours
- Notifications: 12h (reminder), 18h (sender alert), 23h (urgent)
- If unclaimed: Auto-refund sender
- Refund amount: €99.90 (€100 - €0.10 processing fee)
- Processing fee covers: Manual refund work + 24h liquidity lock

Note: No BCH was bought (pull system!), so no exchange fees incurred.


WHAT IF THERE'S A DISPUTE?
---------------------------

Merchant says: "I gave cash"
Recipient says: "I never received it"

Resolution (MVP):
- Escrow investigates (24h deadline)
- Evidence requested: Video, photos, witnesses, GPS
- Default: Favor merchant (they're scarce resource)
- 3-strike system for dishonest merchants
- Future: Community arbitration, dispute pool

Safe confirmation sequence (prevents most disputes):
1. Recipient shows code to merchant
2. Merchant enters code (validates)
3. Merchant hands cash to recipient
4. Merchant confirms "I gave cash" (in recipient's presence)
5. Recipient confirms "I received cash" (in merchant's presence)
6. Both confirmations done face-to-face

Self-auditing through UX design!

==============================================
KEY FEATURES
==============================================

VOLATILITY PROTECTION
----------------------

Problem: Crypto price changes destroy value
Solution: Two-step settlement + pull system

Volatility window:
- Traditional crypto remittance: Hours to days
- Asgaya: 30 seconds (BCH settlement only)

Result: 99.5%+ reduction in volatility exposure


PERMISSIONLESS ACCESS
----------------------

No KYC required because:
- Users already KYC'd by their banks (Bizum, PagoMóvil)
- We're just coordinating existing payment systems
- No new accounts, no new identity verification

Anyone can participate:
- Senders: Just need Bizum account (already have it)
- Recipients: Just need phone number (already have it)
- Merchants: Just need Android phone + Asgaya app


COMPETITIVE PRICING
-------------------

Traditional remittance: 6.49% average
Asgaya: 1% total

Why so cheap?
1. Free P2P rails (Bizum, PagoMóvil - no fees)
2. Market-rate exchanges (Kraken - 0.26%, not 6.49%)
3. No intermediaries (direct peer-to-peer)
4. No regulatory overhead (permissionless)

Fee breakdown on €100:
- Kraken exchange: €0.26 (unavoidable)
- Participants (escrow, merchant, LP): €0.74 (split 3 ways)
- Total: €1.00 (vs €6.49 traditional)


MERCHANT INCENTIVES
-------------------

Why merchants join:
1. Earn ~€0.247 per €100 transaction (0.247% fee)
2. Attract new customers (recipients come to their store)
3. Drive repeat business (recipients become regular customers)
4. No risk (receive BCH instantly, can hold or sell)

Example: 100 transactions/month = €24.70 extra income
In Venezuela (or Honduras, Nicaragua, etc.), this is significant!


SELF-CUSTODY
------------

Users control their private keys:
- Merchants receive BCH directly to their wallet
- No custodial risk (no exchange can freeze funds)
- True peer-to-peer transfer
- Bitcoin Cash ethos maintained


BCH AS SETTLEMENT LAYER
------------------------

Why Bitcoin Cash (not Bitcoin, Ethereum, etc.)?

1. Low fees: ~€0.01 per transaction (vs €5-50 for Bitcoin)
2. Fast confirmation: 10 minutes average, 0-conf reliable
3. Reliable: No congestion, predictable fees
4. Philosophy: Designed for payments (not just store of value)

BCH is the bridge, not the destination. Users never need to think about it.

==============================================
PHASES & ROADMAP
==============================================

PHASE 0: ASGAYA HUSK (MANUAL EVERYTHING)
-----------------------------------------

Goal: Validate core concept with minimal automation

Characteristics:
- 1-2 trusted merchants (personal connections)
- Manual escrow operation (operator monitors Bizum manually)
- Manual BCH purchases (operator triggers Kraken orders)
- No app yet (SMS-based coordination)
- Friends & family testers only

Success metric: 10 successful EUR→VES remittances with 0 disputes
Timeline: Manual, data-driven validation


PHASE 1: MVP (EUR→VES CORRIDOR)
--------------------------------

Goal: First automated implementation

Features:
- Android app (sender, merchant)
- NotificationListener (automated Bizum parsing)
- Backend APIs (19 endpoints)
- Automated BCH settlement
- Dispute resolution framework
- 5-10 merchants in Caracas

Success metric: 100 transactions, <5% dispute rate, <1 minute avg settlement
Timeline: After Phase 0 validation


PHASE 2: SCALE EUR→VES
-----------------------

Goal: Grow Venezuela corridor

Features:
- 50+ merchants across Venezuela
- LP competition (bounty system for instant settlement)
- Merchant onboarding automation
- Dynamic reward modulation (if needed)
- Enhanced fraud detection

Success metric: 1,000 transactions/month, merchant density in major cities


PHASE 3+: NEW CORRIDORS
------------------------

EUR→HNL (Honduras), EUR→NIO (Nicaragua), EUR→Other

Expansion criteria:
- Local payment rail exists (PagoMóvil equivalent)
- Merchant interest validated
- Regulatory environment assessed
- Community demand confirmed

Long-term vision: Global permissionless remittance network

# Asgaya Documentation

> Bitcoin Cash remittances with <1% fees, no KYC, self-custody

## What is Asgaya?

Asgaya is a permissionless remittance protocol that enables cross-border money transfers using Bitcoin Cash as settlement layer, with fiat on/off ramps through local payment systems (Bizum in Spain, PagoMóvil in Venezuela).

**Key Innovation:** Two-step settlement with pull-based BCH purchases eliminates volatility risk.

## Documentation Structure

This documentation is organized into three main sections:

### 1. Core Architecture
**URL:** `/core-architecture/README.md`
**AI Navigator:** `/core-architecture/llm.txt`

Fundamental design principles and economic incentives that make Asgaya work:
- Competitive pricing (<1% fees)
- Volatility protection (pull system)
- Permissionless access (no KYC)
- Self-custody (user controls BCH)

**Start here if:** You want to understand WHY Asgaya is designed this way

### 2. Android App
**URL:** `/android-app/README.md`
**AI Navigator:** `/android-app/llm.txt`

Complete specification for Android mobile app implementation:
- User flows (sender, recipient, merchant)
- Backend APIs (19 endpoints across 5 categories)
- NotificationListener (bridges fiat and BCH networks)

**Start here if:** You want to understand HOW Asgaya works or contribute code

### 3. Research & Analysis
**URL:** `/research/README.md`
**AI Navigator:** `/research/llm.txt`

Deep dives into specific topics:
- RS041: EUR→ARS calculation methods
- RS042: Bizum constraints and workarounds
- RS043: AI-native web architecture (this llm.txt system!)
- RS046: Android app requirements (22+ documents)

**Start here if:** You want to understand the research that led to design decisions

# Android App Documentation

> Complete specification for Asgaya Android application

## Overview

This section contains detailed documentation for the Asgaya Android app, covering:
- User flows (how users interact with the app)
- Backend APIs (19 endpoints the app calls)
- NotificationListener (the "heart of the app" - bridges fiat and BCH)

## Structure

### User Flows
- **Remittance & Merchant Cash-Out** (`/android-app/flows/remittance-merchant-cash-out.md`) - How senders initiate remittances
- **BCH Payment Flows** (`/android-app/flows/bch-payment-flows.md`) - Direct BCH payments
- **Recipient Flows** (`/android-app/flows/recipient-flows.md`) - How recipients receive cash
- **Merchant Flows** (`/android-app/flows/merchant-flows.md`) - How merchants provide liquidity

**Start with:** `bch-payment-flows.md` (simplest, foundation for others)

### Backend APIs
**Navigate:** `/android-app/backend-apis/llm.txt`

19 REST endpoints across 5 categories:
- Rate APIs (1 endpoint)
- Transaction APIs (7 endpoints)
- Settlement APIs (5 endpoints)
- Merchant APIs (4 endpoints)
- User APIs (2 endpoints)

**Key innovation:** BCH signatures for auth (no phone verification, no JWT)

### NotificationListener
**Navigate:** `/android-app/notification-listener/llm.txt`

The "heart of the app" - parses payment notifications to auto-confirm transactions:
- Bizum (Spain) - EUR → Escrow
- PagoMóvil (Venezuela) - VES → Merchant
- OP_RETURN (BCH) - On-chain → All users

**Most critical component** - if this fails, manual fallback required

# Backend APIs

19 REST endpoints across 5 categories.

## Documents

1. README.md - Overview of all APIs
2. rate-apis.md - Exchange rate endpoint (1)
3. transaction-apis.md - Transaction flow (7 endpoints)
4. settlement-apis.md - LP settlement (5 endpoints)
5. merchant-apis.md - Merchant listings (4 endpoints)
6. user-apis.md - User identity (2 endpoints)
7. bch-native-architecture.md - BCH-native design philosophy

## Key Innovation

**BCH signatures for auth** (not JWT, not phone verification):
- User's BCH address = identity
- Off-chain ECDSA signatures for API requests
- No phone verification, no SMS costs
- See: user-apis.md

- Validate state machine logic
- Review error handling and retry semantics

**Security review:**
- Is BCH signature auth secure? (user-apis.md)
- Replay attack prevention?
- Timing attack vectors?

# NotificationListener Documentation

> The "heart of the app" - bridges fiat payment systems and BCH network

## Overview

**NotificationListener** is what makes Asgaya feel magical: payments auto-confirm, no manual steps.

**Three notification systems:**
1. **Bizum** (Spain EUR → Escrow) - Android NotificationListenerService
2. **PagoMóvil** (Venezuela VES → Merchant) - SMS receiver
3. **OP_RETURN** (BCH → All parties) - SPV wallet monitoring

## Documents in This Section

| Document | Purpose | Priority | Lines |
|----------|---------|----------|-------|
| `README.md` | Architecture overview | Read first | 400 |
| `bizum-android.md` | Sabadell Bizum parser | MVP Critical | 874 |
| `pagomovil-android.md` | Venezuelan bank parsers | Post-Beta | 965 |
| `opreturn-spv.md` | BCH notifications | MVP Critical | 1034 |
| `security.md` | Fraud prevention | **Review Priority** | 658 |
| `testing.md` | Testing strategy | **Review Priority** | 751 |

**Total:** 4,682 lines

## Recommended Reading Order

**For understanding:**
1. README.md (architecture overview)
2. bizum-android.md (simplest implementation)
3. opreturn-spv.md (BCH-native approach)
4. security.md (critical review needed)

**For implementation:**
1. testing.md (test strategy - payment flow first)
2. bizum-android.md (implement Bizum parser)
3. opreturn-spv.md (implement SPV wallet)
4. security.md (implement security measures)

## Critical Review Areas

**1. SMS Spoofing Defense** (`security.md`)

**Claim:** Bank shortcode whitelist prevents SMS spoofing

**Question for reviewers:**
- Can attackers spoof bank shortcodes?
- Is telecom infrastructure security assumption valid?
- What about rooted devices or malicious Xposed modules?

**2. Concept Field Verification** (`README.md`, `security.md`)

**Design:** Recipient phone number in Bizum concept field

**Question for reviewers:**
- Is this sufficient as second factor?
- Can attacker guess recipient phone from transaction ID?
- Should we use something stronger (e.g., BCH address hash)?

**3. Fuzzy Format Detector** (`security.md`)

**Design:** Detect bank notification format changes via pattern similarity

**Question for reviewers:**
- Is Jaccard similarity appropriate metric?
- What false positive rate is acceptable?
- Should we use ML instead of regex + fuzzy matching?

## Implementation Status

**Phase 1 (Week 1-2):** Payment Flow
- [ ] Bizum parser
- [ ] OP_RETURN monitor
- [ ] End-to-end test (EUR → BCH)

**Phase 2 (Week 3-4):** Remittance Flow
- [ ] PagoMóvil parser
- [ ] Merchant confirmation
- [ ] End-to-end test (EUR → VES)

# Core Architecture

Core Architecture is the index that connects **requirements** (what and why we need) to **solutions** (how we achieve them). Each section below branches into detailed explanations of the principles, tradeoffs, and implementation approaches.

---

## Documents in This Section

### Economics & Fees
- [why-cheaper-than-legacy.md](core-architecture/why-cheaper-than-legacy.md) - Economic model breakdown and fee structure
- [why-market-rate-exchanges.md](core-architecture/why-market-rate-exchanges.md) - Transparent exchange rates with zero markup
- [why-eliminate-volatility.md](core-architecture/why-eliminate-volatility.md) - Two-step settlement volatility protection

### Adoption & Incentives
- [why-promote-adoption.md](core-architecture/why-promote-adoption.md) - Network effects and participant incentives
- [why-bch-usage-incentive.md](core-architecture/why-bch-usage-incentive.md) - Incentivizing direct BCH payments

### Permissionless Access
- [why-no-kyc.md](core-architecture/why-no-kyc.md) - Peer-to-peer coordination without gatekeepers
- [why-permissionless.md](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md) - Open participation principles
- [why-why-minimal-hardware.md](core-architecture/why-why-minimal-hardware.md) - Support for low-tech solutions
- [why-why-self-custody.md](core-architecture/why-why-self-custody.md) - User-controlled cryptography and keys

---

## How We Satisfy The 3 Core Requirements

### 1️⃣ Cheaper Than Legacy (<1% fees)

**Requirement:** Beat 6.49% average remittance costs through market-rate exchanges and free peer-to-peer rails.

**Architecture solutions:**
- **[Why Cheaper Than Legacy](core-architecture/why-cheaper-than-legacy.md)** — Economic model breakdown: how free rails + market rates achieve <1%
- **[Exchange Rate Safeguard](core-architecture/why-market-rate-exchanges.md)** — Real-time Kraken rates with zero markup
- **[Volatility Protection](core-architecture/why-eliminate-volatility.md)** — Two-step settlement eliminates Bitcoin Cash volatility concerns

**Key principle:** Transparent economics with no hidden markups.

---

### 2️⃣ Promote Adoption

**Requirement:** Every transaction must create economic incentives for participants to join and grow the network.

**Architecture solutions:**
- **[Merchant & LP Incentives](core-architecture/why-promote-adoption.md)** — Fee-splitting model (escrow, merchant, LP each earn from the 1% fee)
- **[BCH Usage Incentives](core-architecture/bch-usage-incentives.md)** — Near zero-fee BCH payments vs. 1% remmitance cash-outs

**Key principle:** the goal is to make asgaya redundant except to interact with the legacy system

**Related concepts:**
- [Dynamic Reward Modulation](concepts/dynamic-reward-modulation.md) — Adjusting incentives based on market conditions
- [BCH Miners as Escrows](concepts/bch-miners-as-escrows.md) — How BCH miners can serve as escrow operators

---

### 3️⃣ Permissionless

**Requirement:** Anyone can participate without KYC, using minimal hardware and knowledge, Asgaya isn't an entity is an open source protocol anyone can participate build and develope

**Architecture solutions:**

**Access & Usability:**
- **[No KYC Model](core-architecture/no-kyc.md)** — Peer-to-peer coordination without central gatekeepers
- **[Permissionless Access](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md)** — Works offline, intermittent connectivity supported
- **[Minimal Hardware](core-architecture/why-minimal-hardware.md)** — From cardboard QR codes, to RFID stickers, to smartphones
- **[Minimal Knowledge](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md)** — Simple UI, Basic fucntions, not crypto jargon

**Security & Self-Custody:**
- **[Self-Custody](core-architecture/why-self-custody.md)** — Users control keys, Asgaya has zero access
- **[Key Education](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md)** — Mandatory backup verification before first use
- **[Key Safekeeping](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md)** — Multiple backup methods for different risk profiles

**Error Prevention:**
- **[Error Mitigation](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md)** — QR codes, guided flows, auto-calculation, auto-complete

**Key principle:** Maximum freedom, minimum risk — no KYC; secure key management where essential.

---

## Design Principles

These principles guide all architectural decisions across the three requirements:

1. **Accessibility Over Perfection** — Serve users with 3% fees rather than refuse unserved markets
2. **Economic Incentives Over Ideology** — Ideology alone has failed to spread the use of BCH lets try rewards inestead.
3. **Simplicity at Scale** — Do a few things excellently rather than many things poorly
4. **Maximum Freedom, Minimum Risk** — No KYC; secure key management education, desigend to avoid errors
5. **Transparent Economics** — Market rates only, zero hidden markups, clear breakdown of cost after each transaction
6. **Low-Tech Compatibility** — Low tech edge cases are given serious consideration

---

## How to Use This Index

**Each architecture document includes:**
- Problem statement and user context
- Why this solution was chosen
- Trade-offs and alternatives considered
- Links to related concepts and implementation decisions

**Start with the requirement that interests you most:**

==============================================
WHY BCH USAGE INCENTIVE
==============================================

# Why: BCH Usage Incentive (Recipients)

**Sub-requirement of:** [Why: Promote Adoption](core-architecture/why-promote-adoption.md)

**Core Requirement:** Recipients must have a reason to keep received funds in BCH rather than immediately cashing out to fiat.

---

## The Problem

### Building "Better Western Union" Isn't the Goal

If 100% of recipients instantly convert BCH to fiat, Asgaya is just another payment rail—cheaper than Western Union, but still trapping users in the legacy banking system.

**This solves nothing long-term:**
- Users still depend on banks for day-to-day spending
- Merchants still deal with bank fees, chargebacks, account freezes
- The banking system still controls who can transact
- We've built a better trap, not freedom

**The actual goal:** Create economic incentives for recipients to hold and spend BCH, bootstrapping a parallel economy where banking system problems don't exist.

---

## Why Recipients Holding BCH Matters

### 1. Breaking the Chicken-and-Egg Problem

**The cycle that kills crypto adoption:**
- Users don't hold BCH → Merchants don't accept it (no customers)
- Merchants don't accept BCH → Users don't hold it (nowhere to spend)

**Traditional approach (ideology):**
"BCH is fast and cheap! Everyone should use it!"
**Result:** 99.9% of merchants ignore it because 99.9% of customers don't have it.

**Asgaya's approach (economics):**
Every remittance creates:
1. A merchant who accepts BCH (earning passive income from remittances)
2. A recipient who received BCH (and sees merchants who accept it)
3. An economic reason to hold BCH (lower fees on repeat spending)

**Result:** Self-reinforcing cycle where each transaction makes the next one more likely.

### 2. Network Growth Requires Retention

**If recipients cash out immediately:**
- 1,000 remittances = 1,000 fiat conversions
- Network size: Still 0 BCH holders
- Growth: None

**If 30% of recipients hold BCH:**
- 1,000 remittances = 300 new BCH holders
- Each holder sees 5-10 local merchants (from remittance network)
- Those holders spend BCH locally (near-zero fees)
- Merchants see more BCH customers, promote acceptance
- **Network compounds**

**The math is simple:** We're not building Asgaya to process remittances forever. We're building it to bootstrap BCH adoption, then become redundant.

---

## The Economic Case

### Scenario: Recipient in Venezuela Receives 5 Transfers/Month

**Option A: Cash out every transfer (stay in fiat system)**
```
Transfer 1: €100 - 1% fee = €99 cash
Transfer 2: €100 - 1% fee = €99 cash  
Transfer 3: €100 - 1% fee = €99 cash
Transfer 4: €100 - 1% fee = €99 cash
Transfer 5: €100 - 1% fee = €99 cash

Total received: €500
Total fees paid: €5 (1% × 5 transactions)
Net: €495
```

**Option B: Hold BCH, spend at local merchants**
```
Transfer 1: €100 → 0.260 BCH (held)
Transfer 2: €100 → 0.260 BCH (held)
Transfer 3: €100 → 0.260 BCH (held)
Transfer 4: €100 → 0.260 BCH (held)  
Transfer 5: €100 → 0.260 BCH (held)

Total received: 1.300 BCH (€500 equivalent)
Spending fees: 5 BCH transactions × ~$0.01 = $0.05 total
Net: €499.95 equivalent
```

**Savings by holding BCH: €4.95/month = €59.40/year**

For someone receiving €500/month in remittances, that's 12% more value by simply spending BCH instead of cashing out to fiat.

### The Compounding Effect

**But wait, there's more:**

If recipient holds BCH and local merchants accept it:
- No bank account required (saves account fees)
- No risk of account freezes or capital controls
- No currency devaluation (BCH is global, not subject to local inflation)
- Instant settlement (no waiting days for bank transfers)
- Can send money to family/friends at near-zero cost

**The value proposition writes itself.**

---

## Why BCH Specifically?

### Practical Requirements for Remittance Settlement

**What Asgaya needs from a settlement layer:**
1. **Low transaction fees** (<$0.01 per transaction, consistently)
2. **Fast confirmations** (seconds for 0-conf, minutes for final settlement)
3. **Proven reliability** (years of operation without critical failures)
4. **Smart contract capabilities** (for covenants, CashTokens, on-chain communication)
5. **Sufficient liquidity** (fiat pairs on major exchanges)

**Why BCH:**

Bitcoin Cash delivers all requirements today, with a proven track record:
- **Fees:** ~$0.001-0.01 per transaction (proven over years)
- **Speed:** Instant for 0-conf merchant payments, ~10min for settlement
- **Reliability:** 7+ years of continuous operation, survived multiple challenges
- **Smart contracts:** Covenants and CashTokens enable advanced settlement patterns
- **Liquidity:** EUR/BCH, USD/BCH pairs on Kraken and other exchanges

**More importantly:** Trust in continued delivery. BCH has consistently shipped the features Asgaya needs (smart contracts, low fees, reliability improvements) without breaking existing functionality.

### Why Not Alternatives?

**Stablecoins (USDC, USDT):**
- Require trust in central issuer (defeats permissionless requirement)
- Subject to regulatory freezes and account blacklists
- No smart contract flexibility for complex settlement

**Other cryptocurrencies:**
- Most have significantly higher fees (disqualifies them for micro-payments)
- Or lack the smart contract features Asgaya requires
- Or haven't proven reliability at scale over years

**The choice is pragmatic, not political:** BCH has the features Asgaya needs, working today, with a community that ships improvements we can build on (covenants, CashTokens, network communication primitives).

---

## Why This Matters for Adoption

### The Vision: Asgaya Becomes Redundant

**Year 1:**
- Users send remittances via Asgaya (cheaper than Western Union)
- Recipients cash out to fiat (old habits)
- Merchants earn passive income from remittances

**Year 2:**
- Same remittance volume, but 30% of recipients hold BCH
- Recipients discover they can pay 10+ local merchants in BCH (near-zero fees)
- Merchants see BCH customers, promote acceptance

**Year 3:**
- 50% of recipients hold BCH long-term
- New merchants join just to accept BCH (not for remittance income)
- Recipients prefer BCH-to-BCH transfers (why pay 1% when you can pay $0.01?)

**Year 5:**
- Most transactions are BCH-to-BCH (not remittances)
- Asgaya's remittance feature rarely used (only for new users onboarding)
- **Mission accomplished:** Created a parallel economy where banking system problems don't apply

**This is the endgame.** Remittances are the kindling. BCH adoption is the fire.

---

## The Honest Pitch to Recipients

**We're not asking you to "believe in crypto."**

**We're asking you to do the math:**
- Hold €500 in fiat bank account: Pay fees, deal with banks, lose purchasing power to inflation
- Hold €500 equivalent in BCH: Spend at 10+ local merchants, near-zero fees, no bank required

**Which costs less? Which gives you more control?**

The answer is obvious once merchants exist. Asgaya's job is to create those merchants by making remittances fund their onboarding.

---

## Related Requirements

- [Why: Promote Adoption](core-architecture/why-promote-adoption.md) — The full adoption flywheel
- [Why: Cheaper Than Legacy](core-architecture/why-cheaper-than-legacy.md) — The fee savings that fund the incentives
- [Why: Permissionless](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md) — Why self-custody and no KYC matter for this vision

---

## Trade-offs and Decisions

See the **Decisions** section for how we achieve this:

- **[Fee Splitting Model](decisions/fee-splitting-model.md)** — Why recipients holding BCH changes the fee calculation
- **[Two-Step Settlement Timing](decisions/two-step-settlement-timing.md)** — How settlement creates the merchant network

---

## The Bottom Line

**Ideology has failed to spread BCH adoption.**

**Economics won't fail.**

Give recipients a clear financial reason to hold BCH (lower fees + more merchants), and they will. Give merchants a clear financial reason to accept BCH (passive remittance income), and they will.

**The network grows because participants are better off financially, not because they believe in a vision.**

---

*Last updated: May 1, 2026*
*Core principle: "We're not building better Western Union. We're building the on-ramp to a parallel economy."*


==============================================
WHY CHEAPER THAN LEGACY
==============================================

# Why: Cheaper Than Legacy (<1% fees)

**Core Requirement:** Beat the 6.49% average remittance cost and achieve <1% fees through market-rate exchanges and free peer-to-peer rails.

---

## The Problem

The global remittance market moves **$685 billion annually**, but **$44.5 billion never reaches recipients** due to fees averaging 6.49%.

This is unacceptable for migrants sending money home to support families. A worker in Spain sending €500 to Venezuela loses €32.45 to fees in the legacy system. Over a year (12 transfers), that's €389.40 lost—nearly a full month's worth of remittances.

Despite UN and G20 pledges to reduce costs to 3% by 2030, progress has stalled. The legacy system has no incentive to change.

---

## Sub-Requirements

To achieve <1% fees, Asgaya must satisfy these technical and economic requirements:

### 1. Market-Rate Exchanges Only

**Requirement:** Use real market exchange rates with zero markup.

**Why this matters:** Hidden markups are where legacy systems extract the most profit. A 2% "spread" on exchange rates costs more than stated transfer fees. Government-imposed rates can extract up to 65% of remittance value.

**Success metric:** Users can independently verify rates match Kraken's published rates.

**Deep dive:** [Why: Market-Rate Exchanges](core-architecture/why-market-rate-exchanges.md) — Government extraction problem, why BCH bypasses financial repression

---

### 2. Eliminate Volatility Risk

**Requirement:** Protect users from cryptocurrency price fluctuations during the transfer.

**Why this matters:** If BCH drops 5% between when the sender commits EUR and when the recipient gets local currency, the entire fee advantage is lost. Volatility risk is why every crypto remittance project has failed.

**Success metric:** <0.5% slippage on 95% of transactions due to volatility.

**Deep dive:** [Why: Eliminate Volatility](core-architecture/why-eliminate-volatility.md) — Why surprise losses kill adoption, who should bear volatility risk

---

### 3. Free Peer-to-Peer Rails

**Requirement:** Use payment systems with zero or near-zero transfer costs (Bizum, PagoMóvil, BCH transactions).

**Why this matters:** Every intermediary charges a fee. Legacy remittances pass through 3-5 intermediaries (local bank → correspondent bank → SWIFT → receiving bank → payment processor). Each takes a cut.

Asgaya eliminates intermediaries by using:
- **Bizum** (Spain): Free peer-to-peer transfers
- **PagoMóvil** (Venezuela): Free peer-to-peer transfers
- **Bitcoin Cash**: ~$0.01 transaction fees

**Success metric:** Total non-exchange costs < 0.3% of transfer amount.

**How we achieve this:** [Volatility Protection](core-architecture/why-eliminate-volatility.md) + [Fee Splitting Model](decisions/fee-splitting-model.md)

---

### 4. Transparent Cost Breakdown

**Requirement:** Show users exactly where their money goes: exchange cost, BCH network fee, participant rewards.

**Why this matters:** Trust. Users need to verify that Asgaya isn't hiding markups like legacy systems do.

**Success metric:** Every transaction shows itemized costs. Users can independently verify exchange rates match Kraken's published rates.

**How we achieve this:** Built into transaction receipts and user flows.

---

### 5. Competitive on Difficult Corridors

**Requirement:** Even on corridors where <1% is unrealistic (due to liquidity constraints or exchange limitations), beat legacy systems by 50%+ savings.

**Why this matters:** Accessibility over perfection. Refusing to serve a corridor because we can't hit <1% means those users stay trapped in the 6.49% legacy system.

**Example:** If a difficult corridor costs 3%, that's still saving €16.23 on a €500 transfer vs. 6.49% (€32.45).

**Success metric:** 50%+ savings vs. Western Union verified on all supported corridors.

**How we achieve this:** Pragmatic corridor-by-corridor approach (see fee breakdown below)

---

## The 1% Fee Breakdown

**How the 1% total fee is distributed:**

**Example: €100 transfer**
- **Total fee:** €1.00 (1% of transfer)
- **Exchange fee (Kraken):** ~€0.26 (0.26% - deducted first)
- **Remaining:** ~€0.74 (0.74% - split equally three ways)

**The remaining €0.74 is divided equally among:**
- **Escrow operator:** €0.247 (0.247%)
- **Merchant:** €0.247 (0.247%)
- **Liquidity Provider:** €0.247 (0.247%) *(if instant settlement selected)*

**Key insight:** Participants don't split the full 1% - they split what remains after exchange costs. This creates economic incentives for all participants while still beating legacy systems by 85% (€1 vs €6.49 on €100).

---

## Why This Requirement Comes First

Cheaper fees are the entry point. Without a clear economic advantage, users won't switch from Western Union or bank transfers. Once they experience the cost savings, they stay for the other benefits (permissionless access, self-custody, BCH adoption).

**The economic case must be undeniable.**

---

## Related Requirements

- [Why: Promote Adoption](core-architecture/why-promote-adoption.md) — How we use fee savings to incentivize network growth
- [Why: Permissionless](core-architecture/why-permissionless.md) — How we keep costs low by eliminating KYC/compliance overhead

---

## Trade-offs and Decisions

See the **Decisions** section for detailed documentation of the trade-offs made to achieve this requirement:

- **[Two-Step Settlement Timing](decisions/two-step-settlement-timing.md)** — Why pull-based BCH purchase eliminates volatility risk
- **[Fee Splitting Model](decisions/fee-splitting-model.md)** — How the 1% fee is distributed to incentivize all participants
- Decision: Why Kraken exchange (coming soon)

---
